10 Best Sci-Fi Reads Better Than Black Mirror

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Charlie Brooker's Black Mirror is technically science fiction, but it's so much more than that. It shines a light on our society and technology in a way that's disturbingly relatable. Watching it feels uncomfortable because, as unsettling as the stories it portrays are, sometimes they're not too far off from the lives we actually live.

If you've binged the entire show and are still looking for more content with the same vibes, there are many science fiction books that feel like reading a Black Mirror episode. They coat social commentary in a tense and unsettling atmosphere. After reading these, you might come away understanding society a bit better than when you started... for better or worse.

Every day, there's an increasingly large number of mouths to feed. In Tender Is the Flesh by Agustina Bazterrica (available for purchase here), the problem becomes even worse when a vius makes all animal meat poisonous to humans.

The solution that the government in the novel comes up with is raising humans as livestock. The protagonist, Marcos, works at a processing plant for this "special meat." When he's gifted a live woman for his consumption, he starts to warm up to her and begins to do the unthinkable: Treat her like a human being.

We live at a time when human beings are increasingly looking to artificial intelligence for companionship. Annie Bot by Sierra Greer (available for purchase here) gives a fresh take to the concept of the perfect computer-powered girlfriend, which has been seen in media and movies plenty of times.

The story is told from the point of view of Annie Bot, the AI woman who was created to be the perfect companion for Doug. She sees to his every whim, keeps the house clean, and tries to give him what he wants.

But the more human she becomes, the less perfect she gets. Slowly, it becomes apparent that Doug might not really know what he even wants, and that Annie Bot might be able to look beyond her role as companion.

Everyday things have been disappearing in The Memory Police by Yoko Ogawa (available for purchase here). It starts with small things, like hats, roses, birds. These objects don't just vanish from the world, but even from people's memories, like they never even existed in the first place.

The protagonist is a young woman who's one of the few with the power to remember the lost objects. This puts her at risk from the overbearing Memory Police, who exist to make sure that the forgotten remains forgotten, at any cost.

Little by little, more serious things start to disappear with horrible repercussions. All the protagonist can do is watch as life changes completely, while everyone around her remains oblivious that anything wrong is even happening: a situation that would feel right at home on Black Mirror.

When considering how technology interacts with society, it's impossible not to mention the classic Do Androids Dream Of Electric Sheep? by Philip K Dick (available for purchase here). This sci-fi novel inspired the Blade Runner series and remains one of the most beloved sci-fi novels.

The book takes place in a post-apocalyptic world that's shrouded in a cloud of radioactive dust, and most of humanity has escaped to Mars. Rick Deckard remains on Earth with his pet electric sheep to do a job: He's an android killer who tracks down and kills rogue robots for the police.

His latest assignment is to track and destroy six androids on the run from authorities. The only problem is that these androids are very smart, and they look and sound exactly like regular people.

There Is No Antimemetics Division by an author known only as qntm (available for purchase here) is an SCP novel. If you're unfamiliar, the fictional SCP Foundation secures, contains, and protects anomalies and strange supernatural phenomena and entities.

The titular Antimemetics Division is a branch of the SCP that deals with anti-memes, or ideas that can't be remembered or spread. These entities mess with people's minds and prevents them from remembering or sharing information about them, obviously making it extremely difficult to trap or contain them.

The story is told over the course of several seemingly unconnected short stories. Eventually, you realize two things: everything is connected, and there's a horrible threat looming over humanity, but no one can remember that it exists. This is a strange and unsettling read that comes together in a very satisfying way and explores the way human beings think and act, much like an episode of Black Mirror.

Many of the novels by John Marrs are Black Mirror-esque and explore a world where technology and science are just a little too far advanced for its own good. The sci-fi novel The One (available for purchase here) is the first entry in the Dark Future collection, and a great place to start if you're looking for your next realistic dystopian fix.

In The One, a new service has rolled out genetic matchmaking. A new DNA test can match you to your perfect partner, who you're the most genetically compatible with. The book follows five people from all walks of life who find out that being matched to their soul mates might not be all it's cracked up to be.

Blake Crouch is a much more action-focused sci-fi novel than many of the others on this list, but Dark Matter (available for purchase here) still feels very much like a story straight from Black Mirror. Jason Dessen is a physics professor on his way home when he's knocked out by a mysterious person. When he wakes up, he discovers that his life is completely different.

His wife isn't married to him, his son doesn't exist, and his work is a lot more significant than his teaching was. In this world, Jason has done something impossible, but he doesn't want the fame and status. All he wants is to go back home to his wife and son. This sends him on an incredible journey, pursued by an enemy that might be indestructible.

The Test by Sylvain Neuvel (available for purchase here) is a short read, but it's one that will stay on your mind long after you've finished it. Written by one of the contributing writers for Black Mirror, The Test asks what it takes to be a good contributing member of society.

The book's protagonist, Idir, is facing the British Citizenship Test: a brief questionnaire of just 25 multiple-choice questions. It begins simple enough, with a few easy questions. But before long, the testing descends into a dystopian life or death situation that will make you question how you value life and what makes a good citizen.

Exhalation by Ted Chiang (available for purchase here) is an anthology consisting of nine short stories. Each tale has the dreamy, slightly "off" feel of a Black Mirror episode, and scenarios that feel like you get not one but nine whole new episodes.

The collection explores moral and philosophical questions. If you had the option to go back in time and fix some mistakes, would you? How would multiple universes change the way we see free will and choice? These profound and speculative stories will make you reexamine what you think about the world and human nature.

The movie version of Kazuo Ishiguro's Klara and the Sun—a story about a solar-poweed, child-like companion android—has recently brought the author's dream-like writing to the public eye. Never Let Me Go (available for purchase here) is another story worth checking out for fans of Black Mirror.

In this book, we spend some time learning about Kathy, a 31-year-old woman with fond memories of her exclusive private school. As she remembers her life as a student, the memories are tinged with nostalgia and an air of unease.

There's something very wrong about the school, that has unsettling ramifications for Kathy's future. If you're a fan of the eerie atmosphere of Black Mirror, this one might be just right for you.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of ScreenRant, directly in your inbox.

Why New Boeing Planes Are Green Before Delivery, But Airbus Aircraft Aren't

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The observation that pre-delivery Boeing aircraft are green, and Airbus aluminum aircraft aren't, is partially true, but it is far from the full picture. There are significant differences in the substances that Boeing and Airbus' various divisions and subcontractors use, and this is partially driven by environmental regulations differing by location. But unfinished Boeing aircraft are also typically photographed at an earlier stage of development compared with Airbus.

After the peelable Temporary Protective Coating (TPC / "Spraylat") is removed, Boeing aircraft look different, but they are rarely photographed at that stage. This is the stage that Airbus aircraft are typically photographed. Importantly, the 'green' conversation requires aluminum aircraft, as composite material structures are treated differently. Here is what to know about Boeing and Airbus coatings.

One of the most distinctive features of new aircraft is the stage they go through when they are in their primary coats of paint. Boeing aircraft are green as they roll out of the assembly facility. The green coating is a corrosion-protective primer applied to the aircraft structure before its final exterior paint scheme. The layer also helps subsequent coatings to adhere to the aircraft. Other aircraft manufacturers such as Airbus, Embraer, ATR, and COMAC use broadly comparable surface-protection coatings, although they are not quite the same.

It is also important to distinguish between the aircraft's three basic stages of coating. Aircraft receive structural primer, intermediate coating, and final decorative topcoats. What looks like a single coat of green paint on Boeing aircraft is actually a combination of two distinct coatings, obviously not the final decorative one. What is seen is typically the Temporary Protective Coating (TPC / "Spraylat") and the Corrosion-Inhibiting Primer (The True Undercoat).

This does not apply to the composite-material-constructed 787 Dreamliner (more on that below). Instead, it is applied to traditional aluminum structures. It is worth noting that while the Boeing 737 is now in its fourth (MAX) generation, its fuselage remains a fundamentally aluminum structure and a 1960s design. Something similar is also true of the heavily updated Boeing 777X soon to enter service. It may come with new wings, but its fuselage remains primarily aluminum.

Boeing's clean-sheet Boeing 787 is its only 'new' aircraft in the narrow sense of being a clean-sheet design able to take full advantage of today's advanced composite materials. The only 'new' Airbus analog is the Airbus A350. The A330neo and A320neo series are upgraded older aluminum fuselage aircraft. The Boeing 787's fuselage and wings are built with carbon-fiber-reinforced polymer (CFRP) rather than aluminum and so do not suffer from galvanic or atmospheric metal corrosion.

Consequently, Dreamliners do not come with green TPC or green chromate primers. As they roll out of the assembly line, they appear white or light-gray/beige, being coated with conductive/UV-protective primer coats. The Dreamliner's primary competitor, the A350, comes with a tan/beige primer coating, which serves a similar function. Sections in white or beige are usually composite materials, which don't corrode and don't need the coating. That's why the composite-bodied 787 looks less green than the aluminum 737.

Stage 1: component fabrication, sub-assembly transit

Stage 2: Final assembly, outdoor flight line

Bare bright silver or matte pale yellow-green

This is perhaps the most striking on the Boeing 777X. As stated, it retained the 1990s-era aluminum fuselage. Accordingly, the fuselage is painted green in the signature glossy emerald-green Temporary Protective Coating (TPC). Meanwhile, the wings are made of composite and so receive a white or light-gray UV-protective topcoat that matches the Boeing 787. Boeing has also applied a strippable green film to its early-built 777-9s, which have been stored outdoors awaiting certification for years.

A closer look at the paint and production processes of Boeing airliners.

Airbus' aluminum aircraft are similar, but also different. These receive chromate-free exterior primers, corrosion-protective coatings, and decorative topcoats. But while the basic process is the same, they are still chromate-free. Airbus started introducing chromate-free exterior primers on the A380 in 2016, almost a decade after the Superjumbo entered service. While the Superjumbo was a clean-sheet aircraft, it was not a 'new' aircraft in the sense of the Boeing 787 and Airbus A350.

The A380 was built using the traditional aluminum-alloy fuselage. One of the criticisms of the program was that it was a big airplane more than it was a new one. Airbus and Boeing operate in different regulatory environments. The European Union has strict REACH chemical regulations that are phasing out hexavalent chromium more aggressively than in the United States.

In 2025, CIRS reported, "the European Chemicals Agency (ECHA) unveiled a proposal to restrict the use of carcinogenic hexavalent chromium (Cr(VI)) substances across the EU. The measure aims to safeguard human health and the environment from the cancer risks associated with these chemicals." This has pushed Airbus to adopt chromate-free primers faster than Boeing. These typically appear tan, beige, or pale yellow. Airbus generally doesn't use Boeing's glossy, peelable emerald green 'Spraylat' transport film. The structural primer is therefore directly visible.

Another distinctive feature of Airbus aircraft is that when assembled, their coatings are a patchwork of shades. Boeing is a national company; Airbus is a multinational company formed from the merger of major European aerospace companies. It has four home countries (the UK, France, Germany, and Spain). Accordingly, it uses a distributed European manufacturing model.

Barrels, wings, and tails are fabricated, anodized, and primed at different sites and at different subsidiaries across these countries. Each facility applies its own corrosion-inhibiting primer before they are shipped to be assembled. Those primers have slightly different chemistries, resulting in different shades. When the aircraft is pieced together, it looks like something of a patchwork of mismatched greens, yellows, and tans.

Major contractors include GKN Aerospace (Fokker Aerostructures) in the UK and the Netherlands, Latecoere in France, and Spirit AeroSystems. Airbus radomes are often matte gray or tan (made of composite materials), the nose and forward base are pale yellow-green, the center fuselage is slightly darker olive or lime green, the belly fairing is tan or beige, the wings are light gray over green primer, and the aft is olive green aluminum or tan/gray in composite areas.

The XWB-97's maturity is becoming an advantage over the rival new GE9X turbofan, helping the program to progress and be perceived as less risky.

Unlike Airbus, Boeing uses a peelable green coating to protect the aircraft's aluminum skin. The aluminum skin is relatively thin, and even minor scratches and indentations can require inspection. It protects against environmental damage during manufacturing and transport. Like Airbus, Boeing has components manufactured across a broad geological footprint, and these require transportation.

While Airbus transports many of its components on climate-controlled BelugaXL aircraft (and ferries), Boeing relies on the Open-Air Cross-Country Rail Transit for its core 737 aircraft. Those fuselages are built in Wichita, Kansas, and need to be transported 2,000 miles (3,200 km) by open railcar to Renton and Everett. Boeing's highly modified Boeing 747 Dreamlifters are used for the 787, not normally the 737. The train route is primarily operated by BNSF Railway, with the journey going through Nebraska, Wyoming, Montana, to Washington via Spokane.

The journey takes around ten to 12 days, with trains taking three to four fuselages at a time (six maximum). In 2018, Aviation Week reported, "In today’s world on the massive airplane you see going down the rail or you see entering the Renton factory the fuselage is already integrated and they are able to start the same day installing the blankets and wiring and going through the production process without having to do any structural work to the airplane."

Another key factor to consider when comparing Boeing and Airbus during their manufacturing process is that they are typically photographed at different stages of their manufacturing cycle. This creates an apples-to-oranges mismatch. Most familiar photos of green Boeing aircraft show them with the TPC peelable coating, as they are on the railcars or parked at Renton/Everett facilities.

Airbus photos are typically taken when they are being unloaded from a BelugaXL or rolling off the Final Assembly Line. They may be conducting their first customer acceptance flights. This is a later stage than the Boeing aircraft, and they typically sport a raw structural epoxy primer that gives them a flat, chalky, matte finish.

Other factors also explain why Boeing and Airbus aircraft may appear different. Boeing aircraft parked in Washington are frequently pictured in overcast skies, while Airbus aircraft in Toulouse are often in warm Mediterranean sunlight. Even the digital camera may react differently. For example, if a planespotter fills much of the frame with a green fuselage, the camera's Auto White Balance (AWB) may automatically shift the image to magenta and warm tones.

Bob's Discount Furniture: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fuel inflation is pressing on Bob's margins, and vendor cost-sharing is the lever management is pulling to hold profit steady.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Bob's Discount Furniture (BOBS -0.53%) announced that net revenue increased 10.3% year over year to $680 million. Store expansion and disciplined overhead management provide a path to sustained earnings growth. Three things management said on the call stand out.

Customers shopping across physical stores and digital channels spend nearly 20% more per order than average buyers. Management uses Omnicart, a digital shopping tool that lets customers start an order in store and complete it online, to capture e-commerce sales without losing physical retail touchpoints.

Management targets 10% annual store growth by opening multiple regional locations simultaneously. Launching several stores at once builds immediate brand awareness while supporting localized infrastructure, such as regional distribution centers.

Adjusted gross margin, the portion of revenue remaining after accounting for direct product and shipping expenses, dropped 30 basis points due to fuel inflation. Management relies on vendor cost-sharing and targeted price adjustments to protect profitability.

Furniture category demand shows signs of stabilizing across income cohorts, allowing the business to capture market share through low-cost operations, disciplined pricing analytics, and its omnichannel strategy, an approach that provides a seamless, consistent customer experience across every touchpoint.

Management is watching fuel surcharges and supply chain costs while preparing for upcoming Southeast expansion.

I’m Obsessed With UGG’s Ugly-Cute Ballet Sneakers (Others Disagree)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Quill Ballet Sneakers have divided the E! shopping team, but their cozy lining and easy styling won me over. Here’s how they fit and what to know before buying.

You thought the fashion world was done with ballet sneakers? Think again! These trendy shoes just got a chilly-weather makeover. Introducing the UGG Quill Ballet Sneakers, the cozy winter slipper meets sturdy sole meets delicate pointe shoe in the brand's signature colorways and raw suede edges. In other words... some people love it, and others hate it.

This UGG ballet sneaker has truly divided the E! shopping team, with various team members swearing they're ugly and others who are absolutely obsessed with them. I'm in the latter camp. Let's face it, we don't usually go to UGG for their trendy pieces (although some other flats in their collection might be changing that), we turn to them time and time again for the cozy & casual look, and that's exactly what this shoe provides.

I got the UGG ballet sneakers in the brand's signature chestnut/sand colorway a month ago... and love them. They're so comfortable and so easy to style. I usually pair them with jeans and a casual top or sweater. They're cute in an "ugly" way. They can be worn with or without socks. I'd recommend them with socks if you're walking a lot, and I even prefer the look of them with thick socks. But wearing them with bare feet is also an easy vibe.

When it comes to sizing, I'm usually between sizes 8.5 and 9. The smaller size is for flats, heels, and everything I'd prefer to wear without socks. 9 is reserved for narrow shoes, boots, and low-profile sneakers that have a slimmer fit. For UGG's ballet sneakers, I went with an 8.5, and they're the tiniest bit too big without socks and absolutely perfect with a thick sock.

If you love the coziness of your favorite UGG slipper with the ease of styling a ballet flat and the sturdiness of a sneaker sole, I'd highly recommend the UGG Quill Ballet Sneakers—especially when certain colors are on sale!

In UGG's classic chestnut/sand colorway, these ballet sneakers feature a soft suede upper, lace-up ties that wrap around the ankle, and an ultra-plush natural sheepskin lining.

Prefer a color that goes with everything in your closet? This black colorway is the perfect neutral, with the same features as the classic shade, but with more subtle raw edges on the suede upper.

Chic, while still a colorful neutral, this high-voltage blue colorway of UGG's ballet sneaker is to die for (in this shopping writer's opinion, at least).

If you want a fiery pop of color, this (nearly sold-out) red pepper colorway of the UGG ballet sneaker is a gorgeous, cool-toned red you won't find anywhere else.

GTA 6 PS5 Codes Potentially Being Stolen from Physical Copies Using Flashlight Trick

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A potentially problematic manufacturing oversight may be resulting in some GTA 6 codes being stolen from retail PS5 cases.

Some reports claim that by pointing a strong flashlight at the side of the case, it’s possible to shine light into the empty Blu-ray box in such a way that it makes the code printed on the insert visible.

Remember, GTA 6’s physical release is a code-in-box, and doesn’t have a Blu-ray disc.

There is some debate about whether the included poster insert blocks the light and makes this impossible; some say they’ve been able to replicate the issue, while others say it can’t be done with a sealed copy.

It’s also worth noting that the US release specifically has a camouflaged pattern around the code, as shown by ResetEra user Mattmo831. That means you can certainly shine light into the box, but you can’t read the code on the other side.

Some PEGI units don’t have the camouflage pattern, so technically the code is visible using this trick, although again there’s still debate over whether the poster blocks the light in sealed copies.

It’s worth pointing out that codes aren’t activated at the point of purchase like other digital vouchers, so assuming the flashlight method works, it would be perfectly possible to pilfer a working copy of the game.

I think this story will explode if it turns out to be true, but it all just depends on how many PEGI copies don’t have the camouflage pattern and whether the inserted poster blocks the light or not.

As far as I can see, there aren’t an enormous number of people who’ve complained upon buying the physical version that their code has been redeemed – but if this method works, the publicity around it will now surely see the probability increase.

Kanye West Settles Lawsuit Over Sample Blocked by Descendant of Holocaust Survivors

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A federal judge agreed Thursday to dismiss a copyright infringement lawsuit against Kanye West after the musician reached a settlement with Alice Merton, the German singer-songwriter who accused West of using a sample of her song “Blindside” without permission on his track “Gun to My Head.”

Merton filed the underlying copyright infringement claim last year, saying she would never grant permission to the artist now known as Ye due to his history of “antisemitic, racist remarks.”

In her lawsuit, Merton described herself as a direct descendant of holocaust survivors who refused to clear the sample because she was “unwilling to compromise her personal beliefs.” Merton said she was “understandably shocked and humiliated” when several websites reported in December 2023 that she appeared to be a willing collaborator on Ye’s song, which featured Kid Cudi and Ty Dolla $ign.

Terms of the new settlement were not disclosed in the order dismissing the action signed by U.S. District Court Judge Fernando L. Aenlle-Rocha and obtained by Rolling Stone. In a Nov. 6 court filing, lawyers for both sides said they had agreed to resolve the dispute and expected to seek dismissal of the lawsuit once the settlement terms had been fulfilled. Attempts to reach the lawyers were not immediately successful Friday.

Ye first teased “Gun to My Head” at an early Vultures listening event in December 2023. The song garnered particular attention because it marked Ye and Cudi’s first collaboration in several years.

According to the lawsuit, “Gun to My Head” sampled a segment of “Blindside” that “spans most of the entire song’s baseline melody.” It also featured a vocal recording of the opening lyric line from Merton’s song, “I sat down with a gun to my head,” which became “the dominant lyric line” in Ye’s song, the complaint said.

Merton said Ye had not contacted her to clear the sample before the listening event, and the song did not appear on the first Vultures album released Feb. 8, 2024. But on Feb. 15, 2024, Ye’s clearance company allegedly reached out to clear the sample, and a few weeks later, Merton denied the request, saying Ye’s “values are contrary to our values,” the lawsuit said. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

When Vultures 2 arrived in August 2024, “Gun to My Head” was not included on the official track list, but it was briefly released on a digital deluxe version of the album. Ye also played it at other listening parties, and it ended up in bootlegged versions available on YouTube, the lawsuit said.

When the song did not make the Vultures 2 cut, Ye’s fans allegedly “blamed Merton for not authorizing” the sample and even sent her “death threats,” he complaint alleged. She said Ye “did nothing to stop the abuse, allowing his fans to intimidate and harass [her].”

The lawsuit was not the first to accuse Ye of using copyrighted music without permission at a listening event. In May, a jury found that he had infringed on the copyright of an unreleased demo by sampling it in an early version of his Grammy-winning song “Hurricane,” which he played for 40,000 fans at a sold-out Donda listening event in 2021.

During the six-day trial, lawyers for the plaintiffs — four musicians who composed the one-minute instrumental “MSD PT2” — argued that their clients were entitled to a share of the estimated $5.6 million Ye earned from ticket and merchandise sales at the July 2021 event at Mercedes-Benz Stadium in Atlanta, as well as a deal with Apple Music to stream the show. Trending Stories Pink Floyd’s Roger Waters Files for Divorce From Fourth Wife Kamilah Chavis After 5 Years of Marriage John Mulaney Has Many Thoughts on the 2026 Rock Hall Inductees — And Picks Next Year’s Class The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Dolly Parton Heals the Soul on Final Recording, ‘Torn,’ a Duet With Bono on U2’s New Album

The jury found Ye personally liable for $176,153 and his company Yeezy LLC liable for an additional $176,153. Two other companies, Yeezy Supply and Mascotte Holdings, were found liable for $41,625 and $44,627, respectively, bringing the total award to $438,558.

A spokesman for Ye called the verdict a “failed shakedown,” saying the plaintiffs had demanded $30 million to settle before trial.

NiCE’s next-generation Cognigy bets the hard part of AI agents is running them, not building them

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GUEST COLUMN by Zeus Kerravala

The contact center has been the low-hanging fruit for the artificial intelligence industry. Ask any information technology or business leader what they want to use agentic AI for, and they will say it’s to improve the customer experience, which starts with the contact center.

However, to date, the industry has spent the past two years arguing over which AI agent is the smartest. That’s the wrong debate. Model capabilities are improving so quickly and are getting so much cheaper that raw intelligence and reasoning are becoming the least differentiated part of the stack.

The real challenge for enterprises is operating a fleet of AI agents reliably across complete customer journeys, with the visibility and control a large business requires. Without that, the human, or worse, the customer, is left connecting the dots among the outputs of the growing number of agents they are communicating with.

That’s the thesis behind the next generation of NiCE Cognigy, which NiCE Ltd. launched this week. The release includes a redesigned Agentic Experience Studio, an AI Assist companion spanning the building, testing and improvement of agents, reusable “skills” that package instructions and tools, turn-by-turn tracing and native voice-to-voice model support.

Default speech-to-text, text-to-speech and large language models are now included in the per-conversation price, with premium models from Deepgram, ElevenLabs and Anthropic available with a few clicks and billed by usage.

Ahead of the launch, I attended an analyst briefing with Nhu Ho, senior product marketing manager at NiCE Cognigy, and with Clement Boutier, director of product management, who leads the team that built the release and owns the platform roadmap.

Here are the important points from this release:

Ho opened with a chart showing how long AI can work on its own before a human has to step in. In 2020, it was seconds; in 2024, it was minutes; and this year it’s roughly 16 hours. The footnote was interesting, as it calculated that figure at a 50% success rate. “Capable and dependable do not always mean the same thing,” Ho said. “That is exactly where the platform matters.”

For CX leaders, that’s a solid benchmark to consider. A 50% success rate is a research milestone. In a contact center, it’s a churn event or a compliance violation. As models commoditize, value shifts to the system around them: testing, tracing, guardrails and deterministic fallbacks. NiCE doesn’t build frontier models, and frontier model builders don’t run contact centers. Positioning Cognigy as the layer that makes AI dependable is a smart place to plant a flag.

Cognigy earned its pre-acquisition leadership position by handling complex production deployments for large brands such as Lufthansa, Mercedes and DHL. But that flexibility and customizability added complexity. Boutier put it this way: “The large number of options when configuring an agent creates friction to get started, which slows things down.” The new studio lets a builder stand up an agent in a few clicks, with models already provisioned, while power-user tools, including full access to the agent’s system prompt, are available in advanced sections.

This is important because NiCE aims to create a “1+1=3” value proposition for its current customers. NiCE needs Cognigy to expand broadly across the CXone installed base, not just among expert teams at the largest enterprises. Boutier described three builder personas: smaller businesses building simple agents, contact center administrators and team leads, and engineers at technically sophisticated companies. AI Assist is aimed squarely at the first two.

In the demo, it built an agent, tested it, fixed the problems it found, and deployed it to WhatsApp. The flip side is that lowering the barrier means more agents built by less experienced people, which makes the governance features the real story of this release.

Despite all the agentic hype, most enterprises aren’t ready to hand over entire journeys to an autonomous agent. Boutier outlined three modes: traditional natural language understanding, hybrid and fully agentic. Hybrid “is actually where most of our customers are,” he said, while fully agentic deployments are at the “beginning of the adoption curve.” This approach lets customers ease into agentic, build confidence and move to fully autonomous at their own pace.

NiCE designed the product for that approach. An agent can serve as the orchestration layer, calling existing flows as tools. Flows can also be placed in front of agents, for example, to scrub credit card numbers or other personal data before any data reaches a model. Ho described the release as “an evolution, not a replacement,” with production deployments, application programming interfaces and integrations continuing to run unchanged.

That’s a meaningful advantage over AI-native challengers whose pitch effectively requires a rebuild. Contact centers are the fastest-moving environments, and deterministic workflows aren’t legacy baggage. They’re how a business enforces what a large language model shouldn’t be deciding.

“You can have the best builder experience, but if you don’t have any feedback loop on what you’re building, you’re never going to build a great agent,” Boutier said. The new tracing shows each turn: which skill loaded, which tool was called and with what arguments, and when the agent handed off to a flow.

The Simulator now supports multivariate testing of up to 10 variants, using an LLM-as-judge to run synthetic customers against an agent. Boutier ran the same agent on two models and found that one was faster and consumed fewer tokens. This shifts model selection from a preference to a data-backed economic decision.

Voice-to-voice support may be the first feature customers deploy. Boutier noted that buyers are open to using a speech-to-text and text-to-speech cascade, “but what they realize is that voice-to-voice models sound just better.” In the demo, the agent delegated tool calls to a separate model while continuing to talk, making the conversation sound far more natural.

Cognigy now serves as both a Model Context Protocol client and server and supports agent-to-agent connections, including one customer linking Cognigy to agents built with Microsoft’s tooling. NiCE is also co-developing the Personal Agent Protocol with Meta and Sierra, which isn’t live yet. Boutier described Cognigy as “the front door” of the contact center, where a consumer’s personal agent must authenticate before it can proceed. It’s great to see NiCE collaborating with Sierra, a direct competitor, on this, as it makes things easier for a customer.

The industry views agent identity as a shared responsibility. But as Sierra outlines, companies can also let personal agents work through their websites and APIs, so the front-door position depends on enterprises routing that traffic through Cognigy. This lets brands interact with personal agents safely.

There has been a wave of companies banning personal agents, which I think is a mistake. If something is good for a consumer, it will happen, so it’s better to be on the leading edge of enabling it rather than fighting it.

This release is less about new capabilities and more about NiCE betting on where value will accrue as AI agents mature. Intelligence is getting cheaper. Dependability, governance and the ability to blend autonomous reasoning with deterministic control are not.

NiCE is the market leader in contact center as a service, as Cognigy was in conversational AI. Now the challenge is to get an easier-to-work-with Cognigy into the hands of thousands of CXone customers without creating a sprawl of individually governed agents. This will allow NiCE to maintain its industry leadership as personal agents arrive. As it does that, the Cognigy acquisition will move from an add-on to a core component of the NiCE platform.

Zeus Kerravala is a principal analyst at ZK Research, a division of Kerravala Consulting. He wrote this article for SiliconANGLE.

'MobLand' Star's New Crime Thriller Officially Removed From Over 800 Theaters

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Helen Mirren has spent decades proving that she can make just about any character compelling, whether she's playing royalty in The Queen or a ruthless crime boss in Paramount+'s MobLand. The Oscar-winning actress has also continued taking on challenging roles well into her eighties, with her latest movie transforming her into one of literature's most fascinating and controversial figures. Unfortunately, the theatrical numbers on her newest movie tell a very different story.

Mirren's new thriller A Talent for Murder has officially lost 842 theaters since its October 23 debut, shrinking from 876 locations to just 34 after earning approximately $1.13 million domestically. The film opened to $717,864 across 876 theaters before suffering a sharp decline in its second weekend, when its theater count fell to 332 and ticket sales dropped 79% to $152,296.

Things deteriorated further during its third weekend, with another 298 theaters dropping the movie and leaving it playing in only 34 locations. That represents a staggering 96% reduction from its opening footprint, despite the film receiving generally favorable reviews.

Directed by Anton Corbijn, A Talent for Murder follows celebrated novelist Patricia Highsmith, the creator of The Talented Mr. Ripley, during her retirement in the Swiss Alps. In the movie, Edward Ridgeway is sent by his employer, a publishing house, to see reclusive author Patricia Highsmith.

It's 1995, and Ridgeway has been tasked with getting Highsmith to sign a contract promising a sixth Tom Ripley book. On the train there, he meets Clementine Balfour, who's on her way to visit her wealthy aunt. When Ridgeway meets Highsmith, sparks fly: She alternately toys with him and threatens to throw him out. When she teasingly begins outlining the new Ripley story, she asks him for a character name. He volunteers the name "Clementine Balfour," and then viewers start to see the new story unfold, as a new Ripley romances Clementine, with his eye on the family fortune. But soon fiction becomes dangerously entwined with life.

The film stars Mirren (The Queen) as Patricia Highsmith, Alden Ehrenreich (Oppenheimer) as Edward Ridgeway, Olivia Cooke (House of the Dragon) as Clementine Balfour, Juliet Stevenson (Bend It Like Beckham) as Peggy, and James Faulkner (Game of Thrones) as Douglas Balfour.

A Talent for Murder opened in US theaters on October 23, 2026, and is now available through digital rental and purchase platforms. Stay tuned to Collider for all the latest news and updates on the biggest and best movies and shows heading to your favorite streaming platforms soon.

Epic's Tim Sweeney says Steam's AI rule is "unfair to developers who use AI to make good games"

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

From the creators of Edge: A weekly videogame industry newsletter with analysis from expert writers, guidance from professionals, and insight into what's on the horizon.

Get sneak previews, exclusive competitions and details of special events each month!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"It's a guilt by association pattern that all developers need to push back on"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney has been in a bit of a one-sided beef with Valve co-founder and CEO Gabe Newell for a while now, lately about Steam's AI disclosure that requires developers to include a label informing Steam users of AI-generated content. Sweeney has had a number of complaints about Valve's storefront over the years, which makes sense as he owns one of its main competitors, the Epic Games Store, but he's been doubling and tripling down on the AI disclosure issue as of late. In a new interview, he not only sharply rebukes the AI label, but embraces AI in game development to degree rarely seen by him or other industry leaders.

Talking to GamesBeat, Sweeney gives one of the most bullish takes on AI generation in games I've seen in my many years covering this topic. Get on-board or die, is essentially his perspective.

"It's really clear to me that large-scale game developers–setting aside indies, where there's always going to be specialties and niches, large-scale game development will be totally changed by AI," Sweeney says. "Teams that don't adopt it will absolutely be left behind and rendered uneconomical, non-viable compared to their peers."

Sweeney says he's against any kind of legislation to curb the use of AI in game development in the US because it would give other countries the advantage. "Everyone is going to be adopting it," he says. "When one team can make a game four times more efficiently – 10 times more efficiently soon, perhaps – than another team can make the same game, there's just no possibility of competing the old way. No game developer has 75% to 95% profit margins that they can put at risk by not being as productive as possible."

There has been massive backlash to AI in games from players, voice actors, artists, and indie developers, but messaging from big studios and publishers generally ranges from cautiously optimistic to vocally and enthusiastically supportive. Meanwhile, a report from GDC found that 55% of respondents said AI was having a negative impact on the industry. Still, Sweeney wants to make sure companies aren't being bullied into choosing human workers over AI.

"Because there's a big resistance movement against AI that seems concentrated on trying to bully developers into not adopting it by attacking individuals, by review-bombing projects and so on, I've been trying to make my views known to as many game developers as possible," he says. "We have to power through this and build awesome games, use maximum acceleration, because it is a survival issue for all the companies in the industry."

Sweeney has called Valve's AI policy "abhorrent," politically motivated, and nonsensical among other things, and we can add another descriptor to the pile today: toxic. When GamesBeat pushes back and suggests Steam's AI label is there to inform players, Sweeney says he doesn't "buy it in the slightest," pointing to Steam's user review system as a means to let players know about "a game's quality, scope, entertainment value and so on." Ultimately, he says AI can be a force for good and bad in game development, and he sees Valve's mandatory disclosure as a judgment on the technology as a whole.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

"You can build good games and bad games without AI. You can build good games and bad games with AI. You can repeat those distinctions for all engines, all development tools, all methodologies, all countries of origin, all teams and so on," says Sweeney. "If we were to identify AI as associated with bad games, that's just unfair to developers who use AI to make good games. It's a guilt by association pattern that all developers need to push back on."

Sweeney argues that Steam's AI disclosure is "being used toxically here, because it's absolutely being used by anti-AI advocates to undermine the reputations of developers and the viability of their projects."

Newell himself has yet to respond to Sweeney's many tirades, but prominent former Valve developer Chet Faliszek hit back in a YouTube video in which he called Sweeney's behavior "AI psychosis." Faliszek also argued that Sweeney was "treating all the players like children" and advised him to "act as a player yourself for a moment and go, 'What would I want to know about a game?'"

Why so many game developers don't want to use generative AI

After earning an English degree from ASU, I worked as a corporate copy editor while freelancing for places like SFX Magazine, Screen Rant, Game Revolution, and MMORPG on the side. I got my big break here in 2019 with a freelance news gig, and I was hired on as GamesRadar's west coast Staff Writer in 2021. That means I'm responsible for managing the site's western regional executive branch, AKA my home office, and writing about whatever horror game I'm too afraid to finish.

HeartFlow: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Operating losses are narrowing as revenue outpaces expenses, and the company's $253 million cash pile is meant to carry it until it turns cash-flow positive.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 12, 2026, HeartFlow (HTFL -19.13%) raised its full-year revenue outlook to between $262 million and $264 million. The company is accelerating commercial adoption of its artificial intelligence diagnostic platform across cardiovascular care pathways.

Expanding adoption and clinical trial milestones offer long-term investors a clear view of sustainable top-line growth. Three things management said on the call stand out.

Revenue grew 57% year over year in the third quarter of 2026, driven by higher utilization of FFRct, an artificial intelligence tool measuring coronary artery blockage, and Plaque Analysis, a software program analyzing arterial vessel walls. Expanding sales of high-margin software helped lift non-GAAP gross margin, an adjusted measure excluding non-cash compensation, to 83.8%.

A one-year study of 6,000 patients revealed that clinicians using Plaque Analysis reduced major cardiovascular disease (CVD) events by 34% compared to standard coronary computed tomography angiography, or CCTA, scans alone. Demonstrating improved patient outcomes encourages health systems to adopt the software across a broader portion of scan volumes.

Management is initiating three clinical trials in late 2026 and early 2027 to evaluate high-risk patients without symptoms, aiming to unlock an additional $6 billion market. Winning regulatory and reimbursement coverage in this population would expand the company's total addressable market, or TAM, to $11 billion.

Operational leverage continues to strengthen as revenue growth outpaces expenses, narrowing operating losses significantly. The company holds $253 million in cash and short-term investments, which management expects will fund operations until achieving positive cash flow.

Structural advantages in proprietary imaging software, growing clinical trial evidence, and expanding health plan coverage protect its competitive position as management focuses on account activations and pricing discipline.

New products like PCI Navigator, a software tool for planning catheter-based heart stent procedures, extend the platform into treatment planning. Clinical data supporting these tools helps embed the software into hospital workflows and drives long-term customer retention.

Gov. Ferguson seeks $10M to help develop Eastern Wash. clean-energy hub

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Challenges, breakthroughs and big ideas in the race to protect our planet. SEE MORE

by Lisa Stiffler on Nov 13, 2026 at 4:32 pmNovember 13, 2026 at 4:32 pm

Gov. Bob Ferguson is proposing $10 million to fund basic infrastructure needed for the Northwest Advanced Clean Energy Park, a master-planned industrial area in Eastern Washington that will foster development of large-scale clean energy, advanced manufacturing and supply chains. The proposal will be included in his 2027–29 capital budget.

If approved by lawmakers, the funding would support sewer, water, electrical and road infrastructure at the park.

“This investment will support thousands of good-paying jobs in the trades and advanced industries, and unlock billions in private investment,” Ferguson said in a statement.

The 1,641-acre site was formerly part of the Hanford Nuclear Reservation, a sprawling federal property along the Columbia River near the Tri-Cities that is undergoing a massive environmental cleanup. Hanford was established during World War II to produce plutonium for the Manhattan Project.

According to the governor’s office, two projects have committed to locating at the park once infrastructure construction is complete: Atlas Agro’s low-carbon fertilizer production plant and the utility-scale Ruby Flats Solar Farm.

State officials said General Matter is also considering the location for producing advanced nuclear fuel for small reactors. That siting could potentially be beneficial for another project planned for the same area: Amazon, X-energy and Energy Northwest are developing plans for the Cascade Advanced Energy Facility, a nuclear plant comprised of clustered, small reactors.

In 2024, the Norwegian company Cenate expressed interest in building a factory at the park to produce lithium-ion battery components, according to the Tri-Cities Area Journal of Business, ultimately withdrew its plans.

The Tri-Cities is also attracting fusion-energy investment. Last year, Seattle startup Avalanche Energy landed a $10 million grant from the Washington State Department of Commerce’s Green Jobs Grant Program to develop FusionWERX, a commercial-scale testing facility for fusion technologies. The project is being developed in an existing Port of Benton building in Richland.

Washington is heading into its 2027–29 biennial budget with a multibillion-dollar shortfall, but officials have not yet settled on a definitive public estimate.

Ferguson has acknowledged the scale of the gap but has not publicly specified an amount. The shortfall concerns the state’s operating budget, which is separate from the capital budget funding proposed for the energy park.

The Climate Pledge is a commitment to reach net-zero carbon emissions by 2040, co-founded by Amazon and Global Optimism in 2019.

Seven years later, the community has grown to over 700 signatories across 62 industries and 49 countries and territories, reducing emissions, scaling emerging climate solutions, and addressing challenges across operations and value chains.

I Was Surprised By Daniel Radcliffe's Take On Giving Harry Potter Advice

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Daniel Radcliffe And Keira Knightley Have The Same Take On Fans Quoting Their Movies At Them

After Dancing With The Stars Disney Night, I Have Complaints About Jenna And Harry

Rupert Grint Reveals His ‘Hope’ For The New Ron Weasley (And I’m Getting Emotional)

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

With the new Harry Potter series getting closer, Daniel Radcliffe has been getting a lot of questions about any advice he has for Dominic McLaughlin. The actor has previously said he didn’t have advice for McLaughlin and only shared advice for the producers. At the time, he asked them to “let [the young cast] be kids still,” especially in the age of social media. However, I was surprised by his take on why he doesn’t want to give advice.

While the actor was promoting his upcoming movie, Steps, he was asked by AP News Entertainment about handing down the wand. The actor doubled down on not sharing advice, but this time he explained why:

I’m really, truly in the business of not giving Dominic advice. He’s got it, he doesn’t need any advice from me. I don’t want to be like a — because then if I say something to you, then the next interview [with him], people will be like, ‘Oh, Dan said you should do this.’ And I don’t want to haunt this poor child through interviews.

It’s understandable that he doesn’t want to put even more on McLaughlin’s shoulders. It’s a little surprising, though I can see why he doesn’t want to put the young actor under pressure to live up to anything. Meanwhile, he’s not the only Harry Potter actor who has been asked to hand down advice. Bonnie Wright, who played Ginny Weasley, shares a similar opinion to Radcliffe when it comes to telling the next Ginny what to do. She has stated, “I think it’s so important for them to be in their world and their interpretation of it.”

This seems to be largely what the original cast thinks, with Tom Felton also veering away from giving advice on how to portray Draco. However, the Broadway actor focused on the sentimentality of his Harry Potter days and advises the new cast to take lots of pictures. It seems that Radcliffe isn’t the only one not wishing to ‘haunt’ the child actors. Though this take on advice is a little surprising, Radcliffe’s reveal that he’s not a big TV watcher is even more so:

I don’t know if I will [watch] honestly. I’ve been in a lot of Harry Potter already and also, not even because I don’t — I don’t watch any TV. I haven’t seen Breaking Bad, and I haven’t seen Mad Men. I haven’t seen some of the real classics. Eight hours is a lot of anything to watch.

I’m not surprised he hasn’t watched his own franchise, but he’s so behind on so many classics. Perhaps he’ll check out the Harry Potter series with his son. Hopefully, after Season 1 airs, someone will ask him. Though I doubt watching the series will change his stance on giving advice.

Meanwhile, this hasn’t stopped a few of his co-stars. Ralph Fiennes gave some practical advice to whoever takes his place as Voldemort: “Practice your long, flowing, robe walk” and advised him not to trip. Additionally, a pair of Weasleys have shared the sweet gesture they gave to the next Fred and George, up-and-coming actors Tristan and Gabriel Harland. Oliver and James Phelps sent the twins sweet notes wishing the next set of mischief makers the best of luck. They declined to give exact details about what was in the notes, stating they were private messages of encouragement.

The Harry Potter series will arrive on the TV schedule on Christmas Day. It is expected to have a weekly release and will be available with an HBO Max subscription.

GQ’s 2026 Men of the Year Party Brought Everyone Out

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There’s no party quite like a GQ party. Who else could bring together Andrew Garfield, Robby Hoffman, Future, and Angel Reese under one roof? But that’s exactly what we did on Thursday night at Los Angeles’s famed Chateau Marmont for our 31st Men of the Year party, presented by Johnnie Walker Blue Label.

Following a dinner honoring our trio of cover stars—Matt Damon, Chris Rock, and Garfield—the real fun began when all three of them, along with nearly every other celebrity in town, descended on the Chateau’s garden to mix it up to the sounds of DJ Yung Tada (plus a late-night set from GQ Breakouts electro-pop duo Fcukers).

I caught Damon at the entrance to the party looking to continue our conversation from his cover story, but our reunion was cut short when Inde Navarette pulled up to say hi and exchange contacts. If the 25-year-old Obsession newcomer was starstruck by the 56-year-old Hollywood hero, the feeling seemed mutual.

Future and Travis Scott rolled up nearly at the same time; Travis, who performed the end-credits track for The Odyssey, told me he was ready to hit the Oscars campaign trail.

In every corner was a cluster of stars from different Hollywood galaxies. Abbott Elementary’s Tyler James Williams and Chris Perfetti made the rounds together, looking nothing like the harried teachers they play on TV. GQ Breakout Owen Thiele and his longtime friend Cazzie David (who gave Owen his first acting gig on her web series years ago) ignored our red carpet instructions to bless us with photogenic poses they’d pre-planned long in advance. When I asked broadcaster Taylor Rooks for an on-the-fly NBA Cup prediction, she gushed about the Celtics and the Lakers. And there was something fun about seeing Anderson .Paak and Diplo chop it up, too.

The friendly embrace shared by Austin Abrams and Skyler Gisondo, noted Good Dudes, is the unscripted team-up the internet goes nuts for. The Resident Evil and Primetime actors later shared that they’d just been on the phone together while getting ready for the party, neither realizing they’d be seeing each other shortly. When I brought up James Gunn and his recent departure from DC Studios, Gisondo called it a “bummer”—he just wrapped the “Jimmy Olsen vs. Gorilla Grodd” series, he said, and hopes that it’ll still come out (and that Gunn continues to write and direct even if he’s not in executive mode anymore).

In other unlikely but endearing encounters: Ayo Edebiri and Thiele excitedly nabbed IShowSpeed for a photo, and Lamorne Morris stopped Anthony Ippolito to give him props for I Play Rocky, counseling him to take it all in. Ippolito told me he’s headed off to Europe soon to continue promoting the film; a certified foodie, he was hunting for Paris recs (I had to put him on to HolyBelly, for the pancakes). I also caught GQ’s Director of the Year Denis Villeneuve, whose eyes lit up when I mentioned his woefully underrated Jake Gyllenhaal thriller Enemy.

Midway through the night I spotted Andrew Garfield and Monica Barbaro, who were considering an Irish exit. Garfield’s forthcoming Artificial (out December 25) is the movie on everybody’s lips, but he told me he’s looking forward to spending the holidays in New York and taking a brief break before what will probably be a busy campaign season for the Man of the Year.

No one captured the evening as succinctly as Robby Hoffman, who described her upcoming TV show as “Curb [Your Enthusiasm] for poor people.” When the conversation turned back to GQ, Hoffman rued the decline of magazine paper quality. But this latest issue got her seal of approval: appropriately glossy, well-considered, and elite to the touch. Though she might have just been describing the party’s guest list.

But even if you didn’t make the cut, you can see how GQ’s Men of the Year 2026 party looked from the inside. ✦

Have you heard? MatPat’s return, Tubi CEO’s next move, and Trisha Paytas’ payday.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Each week, we handpick a selection of stories to give you a snapshot of trends, updates, business moves, and more from around the creator industry.

This week, a beloved couple has a new channel on YouTube, a vaunted exec has a new gig, and a much-memed creator is making bank (and hitting bookshelves).

Welcome back to YouTube, Matthew and Stephanie Patrick. The man known as MatPat and his wife Stephanie haven’t shared many videos since they sold their Theorist brand nearly four years ago. But these crazy kids couldn’t stay offline forever, so they’re back with PatChat, a new channel featuring the couple’s conversations about internet culture. PatChat won’t upload nearly as frequently as the Theorist channels, and that’s ok. Let the Patricks live a little bit!

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

CNN panelist pushes back against Hasan Piker lawsuit. Piker argued that Scott Jennings defamed him by misconstruing statements that were clearly said in jest. In response, Jennings’ lawyers argued that Piker did make those statements, sarcastic or not — so they are looking to have the $72.5 million lawsuit thrown out. If commentators can be held liable for sarcasm, the internet might be in real trouble.

WhatCultureGaming launches new YouTube channel to pursue different types of content. After building a popular gaming hub, the WhatCulture team is trying something a little different with MegaWhat. The plan is to release original, personality-driven content that will give the WC team more creative freedom while still allowing them to feed the YouTube algorithm on their main channel.

Anjali Sud leaves Tubi to take point at SiriusXM. As CEO of Tubi, Sud led the transformation of the Fox-owned service, which is now a major creator hub. (She was also CEO of Vimeo before that.) Now, Sud is becoming CEO of SiriusXM, giving her a chance to work her magic in the realm of digital radio. Mike Bidgoli will take over for Sud as the new top exec at Tubi HQ.

Elevate Communications brings in TikTok vet to expand creator operations. Elevate is elevating Sam Valentine, who previously connected TikTok with luxury fashion brands as the app’s Head of Digital, Social, and Creator Services. He’ll spearhead digital and social partnerships at Elevate, where he will work with clients that are eager to build creator programs in sectors like sports, entertainment, and healthcare.

Meta brings in well-traveled exec to be the VP of its in-house content studio. After a long stint at NBC News, as well as stops at Goldman Sachs and BlackRock, Rich Latour has arrived at Meta. He will be put in charge of a studio that emphasizes Meta’s brand and highlights its impact across various communities and industries.

Trisha Paytas has a book out (and she makes $100,000 a week on YouTube). While promoting her memoir Crying on the Kitchen Floor, which was recently published by Hachette, Paytas told Andy Cohen that her YouTube earnings can hit six figures during a good week. Oh, and she also has a new dating show that is rolling out on Tubi. Slow down, Trish!

Hulu taps Tinx to revive The Circle (now with TikTok voting). The creator born Christina Najjar is taking the hosting role in a new version of the reality competition series that previously aired on Netflix. Tinx will have big shoes to fill replacing previous host Michelle Buteau, but with help from the voting audience, she can ensure that the Circle revival doesn’t miss a beat.

The first Clio Creator Awards will fete Dhar Mann. Actor Terry Crews will host as the notable advertising award ceremony expands to the realm of creator content. Dhar Mann, YouTube moralist and engineer of many a brand partnership, will receive an inaugural honorary award at the new ceremony.

Kind works with a Savannah Banana to sell a TikTok-exclusive foodstuff. Baseball player Jackson Olson has amassed a significant TikTok following after getting a roster spot on the best-known “Banana Ball” team. Olson is now parlaying his influence to sell a new type of Kind bar that celebrates World Kindness Day — and is only available for purchase via TikTok Shop.

GTA VI codes are flying around on Kick and Twitch ahead of launch day. All of the major platforms want creators to play GTA VI on their services once the game launches, and giving out free copies is a good way to engender that loyalty. At this point, I’m wondering if GTA VI will be able to live up to its immersurable hype, or if viewers will tire of it after it because the main topic of conversation everywhere.

Barbie and Hot Wheels are on TikTok Shop this holiday season. TikTok continues to onboard name brands as it prepares for its most lucrative holiday season shopping spree. So if someone on your list has their eyes on some Mattel products, deals on those items will be hitting TikTok Shop in no time.

Celebs are consenting to having their voices cloned for AI ads. Ogilvy and CAA have teamed up for a partnership that will make a-list talent more accessible for advertisers and agencies that rely on generative AI pipelines. George Clooney is a CAA client, but if he goes along with this, I’m going to have no choice but to start calling him George Cloney.

Shaq is good at dunking, but can he hide in plain sight? The Big Diesel has a new gig: An ABC series that will ask contestants to hide the 7’1″ goliath in plain sight. I think, to be fair, the contestants should get to start by hiding Kevin Hart before working their way up to Shaq.

Another impulse spending deterrent is taking off in China. The WeChat mini-app dubbed ‘Wallet, Please Wait’ lets users decide whether strangers will be able to hold off on big purchases. I would be more interested in app that lets me vote on whether a stranger should be able to tell a stranger how to spend their money.

Martin McDonagh Calls Sam Rockwell “One of the Best Actors of His Generation, If Not the Best”

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Director Martin McDonagh has become as well-known for making memorable films as he is for writing the complex male friendships within them.

"I like mining down into the sensitivity that I think a lot of male relationships do have, but aren't as – especially in American films – explored sensitively," he said about the subject during a panel conversation at the inaugural Vanity Fair Screening Room at LACMA on Friday. "It's not just a buddy film, and it's not a romance, and it's not just about cool CIA guys," McDonagh continued. “It's sort of the opposite in other ways. And that I think is interesting to explore because I think my male relationships are that way. They can be sensitive and sad and happy and all the things that a female relationship can be. So I think that's why I keep coming back to it.”

Martin McDonagh at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

The conversation, which featured McDonagh and the film’s stars John Malkovich and Mariana di Girolamo, was moderated by VF's Senior Hollywood Correspondent Rebecca Ford following an exclusive screening of their new film, Wild Horse Nine. The acclaimed dark comedy follows two CIA agents (Malkovich and Sam Rockwell) who are not only behind the plan to overthrow the Chilean government in 1973, but have also had a hand in several other significant historical moments in the US and abroad.

Maria di Girolamo, John Malkovich and Martin McDonagh, in conversation with Rebecca Ford at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on Friday, November 13, 2026.

Malkovich and Rockwell's characters engage in pithy, often hilarious banter throughout the film, and their onscreen chemistry is a highlight.

John Malkovich at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

"I love Sam," said Malkovich. “I knew him a bit socially before this, but I'd worked with him once before, which was a great pleasure on a film called A Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy. He's like a kind of expert trainer at SeaWorld who can get the whales and the sharks and the dolphins to show things in exchange for a fish. And Sam is a great student of acting. He is a great student of film and of theater, and he's great fun, very hardworking, and just an ideal colleague. I couldn't imagine a better colleague than Sam and I've had some excellent colleagues.”

"He's one of the best actors of his generation, if not the best," adds McDonagh of Rockwell. "He's always surprising, always dangerous, always sensitive, always smart, always coming up with new stuff. And I think there's a danger to him that's lovely to write for. We always hope the characters will always be surprising, and with Sam's voice in the head, that really helps."

Mariana di Girolamo at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on November 13, 2026

For rising star Mariana di Girolamo, a Chilean actress making her English language movie debut with Wild Horse Nine, she was excited and only slightly intimidated to be a part of the cast.

"It was quite a challenge to perform for the first time in English," she said. "I prepared myself with a coach and we rehearsed with John, Sam and Parker [Posey] and Ailin [Salas] in a public theater in New York where I heard the script for the first time. That was amazing for me, for us." di Girolamo, who is very familiar with the fraught period of Chilean history depicted in the film, said she appreciated how McDonagh put it all in context. "It was my first time approaching that period of time in this way with this very beautiful dark humor. I was laughing about these things and feeling empathy with these CIA agents that committed a lot of horrific, horrific things. I've never read something like that, and the arc of my character is so beautiful, unique, I really wanted to be part of this."

Maria di Girolamo, John Malkovich and Martin McDonagh, in conversation with Rebecca Ford at Vanity Fair's Screening Room at LACMA on Friday, November 13, 2026.

The inaugural edition of Vanity Fair's Screening Room pairs this year’s most compelling films with a slate of programming that includes post-screening panels and conversations with actors and filmmakers. The invitation-only events are welcoming an audience from the worlds of film, fashion, culture, politics, media, and tech, much like the magazine itself. VF editor Daniel Kile introduced the first screening, welcoming the audience to the Steve Tisch Theater at LACMA, which is not yet open to the public.

Following the program in Los Angeles, Vanity Fair’s Screening Room will arrive in New York, where screenings and events will take place at Cherry Lane Theatre from December 4–6.

Fallout 5 and Fallout Remasters Officially Announced

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xbox wants more Fallout, and after a yearslong drought of new games, Bethesda Softworks is making it happen. Bethesda plans to release remasters of both Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas for modern platforms, the company said in July in a massive roadmap of the developer's future. Those remasters are part of a larger plan to reinvest in Fallout games — including Fallout 5, which is currently in pre-production — and a new untitled project at Obsidian Entertainment.

"We have multiple Fallout projects in active development right now," Bethesda said in an official announcement this summer. That includes Fallout 5, which is described as the studio's "long-range destination," and "a major Fallout 76 expansion" planned for 2027 called Raven Rock, which will serve as a prequel story to Fallout 3.

Bethesda also confirmed two long-standing rumors, announcing remasters for Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas. The studio did not provide any further details or a release window, but the projects have reportedly been in the works for many years. The existence of a Fallout 3 remaster dates back to at least 2020, when the project's existence was revealed through court documents related to the Federal Trade Commission's (FTC) litigation regarding Microsoft's buyout of Activision Blizzard.

Fallout 3 and Fallout: New Vegas' remasters follow last year's release of The Elder Scrolls 4: Oblivion Remastered, which was co-developed by Virtuos and Bethesda Game Studios. Bethesda also marked the 10-year anniversary of Fallout 4 in 2025, with the release of Fallout 4: Anniversary Edition. That rerelease included the original game, all six of the previously released DLC add-ons, as well as over 150 items pulled from the Fallout 4 Creation Club.

Fallout 3 was originally released in 2008 for PlayStation 3, Windows PC, and Xbox 360. The game was the third major installment in the Fallout series, and the first to be developed by Bethesda after acquiring the rights to the franchise from Interplay Entertainment. Fallout 3 is set in and around Washington D.C. 200 years after a devastating nuclear war, and players control a 19-year-old Vault dweller in search of their missing father.

Fallout: New Vegas followed in 2010, and was developed by Obsidian Entertainment. It was set in the Mojave Desert and casts players as a courier who becomes embroiled in a conflict between various factions who are vying for control of the region.

In addition to new Fallout games, a third season of the Fallout TV series is in the works and recently wrapped filming. Bethesda Game Studios is also hard at work on the next game in its fantasy role-playing game series: The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel is now in development.

SpaceX launches Starship in key test flight of massive rocket

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a key test flight on Friday, SpaceX tried but failed to recover both stages of its massive Starship rocket.

The rocket lifted off from the company's South Texas launch facility at 4:47 p.m. local time, or 5:47 p.m. ET. SpaceX had to abort its plans to catch and reuse the booster, instead diverting it to splash into the Gulf of Mexico, where it burst into flames.

Test Flight 15, the most ambitious Starship mission to date, was the first time Elon Musk's company attempted to catch both the booster and upper stage, or spacecraft portion, of the massive rocket.

Earlier on Friday, SpaceX obtained authorization from the Federal Aviation Administration. Hours later, the FAA issued a nationwide ground stop after reporting communication problems affecting "multiple" air traffic control centers. The SpaceX license wasn't effected by the flight stoppage, a spokesperson for the FAA told CNBC.

The upper stage of the Starship continued its trajectory into orbit after the company failed to catch and salvage the booster. The rocket is expected to fly around the Earth fourteen times in total. If successful, SpaceX will land the upper stage back at its Starbase facility on Saturday.

Starship's success is critical to SpaceX and its ambitions to eventually lift cargo and people to the surface of the Moon and Mars. In the near-term, SpaceX needs to send more satellites into orbit to expands its Starlink satellite internet service, which is currently the company's profit center.

The company's constellation already includes over 11,000 active satellites in low Earth orbit. One Starship rocket has the capacity to carry up to 60 Starlink v3 satellites into orbit, the company has said, representing about 20 times the capacity of earlier Falcon 9 rockets.

In the previous Starship test flight, the company released 26 v3 satellites, and released another 26 in the Friday test flight, with the goal of "Starlink becoming 51% of the internet," SpaceX said in a post on X.

When it comes to launches, "SpaceX is likely a full decade ahead of peers on this front," William Blair analysts wrote in a note out on Friday. "Even if tonight's catch is unsuccessful, a delay by a few months is not that consequential."

Meanwhile, SpaceX is facing fresh criticism over restrictions imposed on its Starshield satellite internet service in Ukraine. Starshield is built on Starlink technology and is the military-tier of SpaceX's satellite internet service.

Earlier this week, Ukraine President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said his country's military forces had experienced limits on Starshield without warning again, harming their ability to defend against Russia's ongoing aerial attacks. Similar limits on Starshield in Ukraine were temporarily imposed last month.

Some U.S. senators have condemned the restrictions, and urged Congress to investigate SpaceX.

Thailand SEC proposes 5 million Baht daily cap on stablecoin transfers — Why?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) has proposed a set of new rules to regulate stablecoin transactions conducted through licensed digital asset operators.

Announced on 12th November, the draft notification aims to prevent cryptocurrencies and stablecoins from being used for money laundering, cybercrime, and bypassing regulations governing international money transfers.

Here, it’s worth noting that this update came soon after September’s public consultation. During the same, the regulator collected feedback from industry participants.

As it stands, the SEC has incorporated those changes into the latest draft. It is now seeking further public comments until 26th November.

In this, one of the key proposals is to introduce a daily transfer limit of 5 million Thai baht per customer at each regulated digital asset operator. That said, the limit would apply separately to incoming and outgoing stablecoin transfers. However, the value of these transactions must also be consistent with the customer’s declared income and financial position.

However, it’s important to note that transfers between a customer’s own accounts at two SEC-supervised operators would be exempt from the limit, provided both operators follow the Travel Rule.

Simultaneously, the SEC also wants to strengthen wallet ownership verification. In this, the SEC has enabled customers to deposit stablecoins from their own verified wallets and withdraw them to wallets registered in their names.

Additionally, operators would also need to follow the Travel Rule, analyze customers, identify suspicious accounts, and track transactions connected to high-risk wallets.

These requirements would be done in order to make it harder for criminals to move illicit funds through regulated platforms.

Finally, the proposal also introduces stricter responsibilities for exchanges and brokers. Exchanges would have to publicly disclose their market makers and the digital assets for which they provide liquidity. Meanwhile, brokers would be prohibited from using liquidity providers for stablecoin-to-Thai baht trading pairs.

Besides that, brokers and dealers offering off-platform transactions would face a minimum transaction threshold of 3 million baht and would have to publish trading prices for transparency. Brokers would no longer be allowed to arrange off-platform trades directly between customers.

Taken together, these steps would help the SEC combat crimes that are concerning the stablecoin ecosystem in Thailand.

Ishika Kumari is a Crypto Analyst at AMBCrypto, specializing in regulatory developments, market dynamics, and blockchain’s real-world impact. She breaks down complex protocols and legislation into practical, easy-to-understand insights.
